### 3.5 Each command keeps its own place

Folding statements into fewer flights must not change what each one sees
or locks:

- **The owner fence is its own command and comes first.** It is the first
  lock of every writing transaction (parent §5.1). A single statement that
  joins `_owner` and `_head` can let the planner take the head lock first,
  which parent §5.1 changed because that order deadlocks with takeover.
- **The receipt re-check is its own command, after the head lock,** for
  the same reason as the generation check below: the receipt a concurrent
  copy committed while this transaction waited for the lock is visible only
  to a later command.
- **The generation check is its own command, after the head lock.**
  Writers run READ COMMITTED (`store.rs:98–101`). Adoption locks `_head`
  and then publishes `_backend.generation` (`opening.rs:105–125`). A
  statement that both locks `_head` and reads `_backend` takes its
  snapshot before it waits for the lock, so it can miss an adoption it
  waited on. A separate later command takes a new snapshot and sees it.
- **A failed fence stops everything after it on the server.** A
  `SELECT … FOR KEY SHARE` that returns no row raises nothing. The fence
  takes the key-share lock and then raises explicitly on a token mismatch,
  for example through an attach-installed function. The commands after it
  are locks and reads, never writes, so an aborted flight leaves nothing
  behind.
- **Every check that decides whether to commit happens before COMMIT is
  sent.** Some are statement errors the server raises: the fence, unique
  and check constraints, deferred-rule failures. Others are results the
  client inspects: the tail's committed-row count must be exactly one
  (`tail.rs:62`, `:80`), released savepoints and member dispositions must
  match the frame, and the program's outcome must be a commit. A flight
  that folds any of these either makes the server raise on it, or ends at
  that check and lets the client inspect the result before the next
  flight. The count includes that wait. Fault tests cover a zero-row tail,
  a statement failure inside each folded flight, and an ambiguous COMMIT.
- **COMMIT is its own flight.** Today a failure before COMMIT is sent is a
  definite abort (`ownership.rs:129–131`). If COMMIT shared a message with
  earlier statements, a lost message would be outcome-unknown even when
  the server never saw COMMIT. Folding COMMIT needs that wider window
  accepted in the parent's retry table first (§5.2), and this RFC does not
  propose it.

## 13. G4a read refinement (2026-10-23)

The accepted controls design's G4a is commissioned in lane `sb4-pg/g4a`.
Short HTTP admission now has 30 executing workers and 98 bounded waiting
slots; queued work owns no database connection. The original absolute body
deadline and intake age survive the queue; expiry and overflow return the
existing non-draining rate-limit refusal. The matched reader/total database
caps remain 30/32. This replaces the root's historical no-extra-queue note
for short requests only. Burst/expiry witnesses and unchanged RT0 tables are
recorded in [the G4a report](../snapback4/reports/20261023-g4a-reads/REPORT.md).

G4a resolves open question 1 for `/q`'s **bounded POST body**: its ingress
uses only parsed/carried facts, with no database call or authorization.
Ordinary capability hints are bounded to 1,024 entries per server/store and
never grant authority or authenticate metadata. Cold credentials use the
existing discovery statement after body intake, then the fresh query
snapshot; action/custom resolution stays uncached. The final shared Rust
resolver compares principal, kind/scope and store binding. Warm W1/W2 fall
to 2/1 PG flights (SQLite 5/5 statements); unknown tokens retain one PG
flight. Existing authority witnesses and both lowered ratchets pass; the
report records cold discovery and the separate added carried-hint witnesses.
This narrowly replaces §7's no-cross-request-facts rule for non-authorizing
ordinary hints; fresh final authority remains mandatory.

G4a's distinct W3-shaped RT0 baseline, after ingress refinement and before
fact composition, is 134 warm PG flights / 132 SQLite statements (138/132
first use). The 32 membership/room-total/unread shape includes an excluded
33rd room; it retains the original W1/W2 and mutation classes. It does not
reuse the M1 harness's different census. Both ratchets pass.

The bounded physical collector subsequently reduces warm W1 and the new W3
class to **one PG flight each**, alongside W2 at one. It fetches ordinary
authority/identity/head/generation and the entire admitted read closure in
one SELECT; the ordinary Rust resolver/interpreter still decides rules,
arithmetic, refusals, meters and cursors. The shared exact cache replays the
same logical read charges and prevents redundant prefetch. Physical admission
reuses the 4 MiB prefetch envelope with 16 access nodes and 32 prepared shapes;
unsupported or unproved forms retain ordinary access. Identity-dependent SQL
address extraction declines JSON/asset-bearing sources and NUL enums, keeping
valid unrelated JSON NUL on the portable Rust decoder. This is access batching,
not B3, a result cache or an authority grant. Both-store meter/outcome and
planted-fact oracles, coherent-cut/revocation and NUL witnesses are in the
G4a report; full qualification is recorded there separately.

The serving statement-fault witness covers every BEGIN/facts/ROLLBACK
boundary of composed reads and retains the original sequence boundaries
using a genuine conservative lifetime-table fallback. Each injected error
must preserve the complete store census and prevent the later-command
sentinel. The 19-test literal/serving suite passes after adapting its stale
expectation of W1's removed second flight.


G4a fix round 1 (2026-10-24), rebased onto RT6 fix round 2, distinguishes
point accesses from ranges even for empty `.id` prefixes. A unique primary
point retains a separate exact-prefix fact for metered lookup; neither fact
can satisfy a wider page. Unknown nodes, incomplete keys/images, malformed
row payloads and malformed hex return the ordinary fallback without indexing
unchecked vectors. Dependent fields with defaults or optional types decline
composition before execution, retaining Rust's sparse-image materialization.

Before SQL parses an address source, a conservative text check for the
canonical NUL escape declines that image and the whole speculative payload.
It preserves stored bytes, including escaped-backslash spellings that merely
cause conservative fallback. Fixed NUL keys decline at admission. Current
text ingress already rejects control U+0000; the new oracle specifically
covers retained canonical text images which ordinary PostgreSQL and SQLite
can read. Any speculative statement failure rolls back its transaction,
discards every speculative fact, and resolves authority/rows in a fresh
ordinary snapshot. A real SQL-error witness revokes the principal between
rollback and reopening, then requires `E_AUTH` rather than old authority or
a speculative SQL error. Existing read-fault boundaries now demand complete
ordinary outcomes after failed composition; sequence/writer errors retain
their original refusal and no-later-command checks.

A separate timer expires queued short requests while every request worker
is blocked, using the earlier original intake/body deadline. It owns no DB
reader. One worker's exit preserves the queue for healthy workers; loss of
all workers refuses it immediately. The 98 queued sockets are added to the
existing 128 non-wait HTTP connection reservation, so small long-wait
configurations retain mutation room. Database caps and the 30/98 execution/
queue limits remain unchanged. The Postgres script explicitly runs ignored
closure oracles and the commit/revocation cut; its evaluator uses a separate
Cargo target namespace instead of deleting shared build artifacts. Both RT0
tables remain byte-identical to the commissioned G4a baseline.

The public Postgres witness uses the commissioned 22 GiB disk stop floor,
matching the lane monitor, instead of its former unrelated 40 GiB constant.

Round-1 qualification passes build, full core and binary, both unchanged RT0
ratchets, the complete Postgres script (1,184.70 s, including ignored closure
oracles/revocation and public serving) and root ref-check. Both private PG16
masters are stopped and independently absent; no owned processes remain.
Detailed before/after evidence is retained in the commissioned external
`rt6g4a` directory; the append-only journal records counts and cleanup.
### K0-Q implementation note

The serving writer now belongs to one named `snapback4-committer` thread.
`engine::command::Command<T>` owns a `Send + 'static` body and receives the
worker's `&mut Engine` at execution. `Queue` carries erased commands on a
bounded FIFO `sync_channel` and returns typed, one-shot acknowledgements.
A queued command cannot borrow the submitting engine or return a transaction
borrow. The same `Engine` and `AnyStore` path serves SQLite and Postgres.
Test-only request counters capture a shared atomic handle at submission and install it for the command body and synchronous continuation, clearing it before the next command so unrelated maintenance and lease renewal stay uncounted.

Serving commands own their operation, arguments, context, principal, ids and
HTTP admission inputs. The admission structures retain credentials and trusted
request facts without retaining sockets or body readers; live session, generation
and authority-clock checks run again against worker state. Password derivation,
HTTP body/response I/O, asset byte I/O and detached query execution remain on
submitting threads. Ephemeral execution uses its existing hub lock and detached
reader, outside the mailbox. Session and receipt checks and reader checkout for
ephemeral requests also use detached readers; a barrier test requires both a
query and an ephemeral mutation to finish before a held durable writer is
released. The in-memory fixture fallback executes on the
worker because that store has no detached connection; production `--memory`
continues to use its private WAL file.

`Shared.engine` is a mailbox handle rather than `FairMutex<Engine>`. Production
has no engine guard to lend a request. Identity writes, mutation preflight and
execution, request-driven publication and jobs, seed/reset, activation, and the
other serving engine accesses run as owned commands. A mutex-backed fixture
adapter exists only under `cfg(test)` for the existing fault/barrier tests.
Detached readers, their epoch replacement, and the ephemeral hub remain separate.
Background turns still defer a short idle request burst, enter the FIFO and
choose the existing contention budget; a continuation's follow-on engine member
executes on the same worker through the existing member driver, without waiting
on its own mailbox.

The mailbox holds **128 waiting commands**, plus the running command. HTTP
submissions and bounded maintenance turns use `try_send`: a full mailbox refuses
before running its body, with the existing HTTP **503 / E_RATE_LIMIT / limit**
refusal and `Retry-After: 1`. Fixed host-control/inspection calls use the same
bounded mailbox and wait at most **100 ms** for space, enough for a short
scheduling burst. Admitted commands wait without an acknowledgement deadline:
a started command delivers its real result, including continuations, however
long COMMIT takes. The former 30-second timeout manufactured unknown outcomes
for successful writes. Lease loss still refuses queued, not-yet-started work
immediately through the independent watchdog. Worker disconnection before
`start_execution` is `Stopped` / `E_COMMITTER_STOPPED`: **not applied**, retryable
after recovery; disconnection after start is `E_COMMIT_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`, requiring
receipt/key reconciliation. There is no unbounded second command queue.
No accepted write is cancelled when its acknowledgement receiver disappears;
no unknown command is automatically retried.

Both `call` and `submit` refuse a submission made by their own committer worker
before creating an admission ticket or enqueueing: typed `SubmitError::Reentrant`
(`E_COMMITTER_REENTRANT`, HTTP 500, non-retryable/not applied). Command construction
also debug-asserts this invariant after the public admission check. Use the inline
member driver for continuations. The fix-forward production-body audit found no
current same-queue reentry; a real-queue witness covers both doors, an empty
admission registry, subsequent queue progress and a call to a different queue.

Clock-control serialization keeps the 100 ms admission bound and overload
response. Its maintenance pause belongs to the request and clears when that
request ends. The regression holds the writer beyond the former 30-second
deadline, verifies no premature response, then observes one successful advance.
After time advances, a maintenance/report refusal returns **500
E_CLOCK_REPORT_INCOMPLETE**, non-retryable, carrying `advancedMs` and
`reportIncomplete`, with no `Retry-After`. Inspect the clock or drain with
`0ms`; repeating the original relative duration deliberately advances again
because the clock door has no idempotency key. A deterministic mailbox-full
witness exercises this recovery without adding another second.
The request retains the published effective `now` separately from the worker's
acknowledgement. Losing that acknowledgement after publication returns the same
incomplete-report envelope, including `now` and `advancedMs`.

Unknown supervised-child mint outcomes fence the original `worker_revision`,
like failed retirement, before a replacement can be minted. Only overload and
known pre-execution refusals retry at the same revision. Source watchers keep
watching after refusals, retry transient non-admission and never re-adopt an
unknown source digest, including after a stored-generation change; later source
edits remain eligible.
The fence applies to an inner `CommitOutcomeUnknown` inside a successful queue
reply too. An injected post-COMMIT unknown through the actual `DevWorker` tick
and queue leaves a durable orphan that cannot authenticate; both named and
conventional worker credentials have this witness.
An uncertain conventional mint also retains retirement custody: its single
stored family must be revoked before replacement overwrites that marker.
Failed retirement suspends replacement and retains the fence. The witness holds
that retirement behind an external writer, then verifies the orphan stays
unauthorized after the real supervisor starts a replacement. Named families
retain their independent revision markers.

A detached `/q/` reader that encounters `E_GENERATION` is discarded and reopened
behind the adoption command, at most twice. This covers adoption COMMIT before
reader replacement without weakening the generation check: a genuine foreign
activation still refuses. The deterministic witness pauses exactly in that gap
and requires HTTP 200 with the original data and new generation. This race
predates K0-Q; the lifecycle replay retains its 1,600-row/62-edge assertions.
Every attempt decodes the original wire arguments against its executing program,
including the in-memory fallback, so a signature-changing handoff refuses stale
arguments. A query-outcome `E_GENERATION` after both handoffs returns HTTP 503,
matching metadata and reader-open failures. Deterministic adoptions witness both
the changed signature and three consecutive query-outcome generation failures.

The K0b/K0c transaction driver is unchanged: every actual member keeps its own
physical transaction, savepoints, explicit disposition, SQL, receipt and error
policy. Each operation's existing engine continuations run on the worker after
its durable COMMIT, then the worker acknowledges its result. Serving mutation, action, effect, route and maintenance wakes, and memory-sync
publication run inside their original admitted command before acknowledgement;
notification cannot itself be refused as a second mailbox item. Wake conditions
and metadata remain attached to that command's result. Request generation,
development refusal evidence, opt-in trace events, queue/engine wall time and
worker CPU measurements return to the submitting request before encoding.
There is no multi-member batch, fill window, overlay, pre-execution or new durable
revision schema in this slice. The remaining direct-opener census is unchanged.

After rebasing onto S10d-2, a queue can retain the acquired Postgres lease.
The host opens the engine with `open_postgres_owned` and installs renewal
before opening its listener. Renewal runs on its dedicated connection and
thread, outside the mailbox. Shutdown drains accepted work before releasing
the lease. The internal Postgres HTTP witness now follows this positive-token
path; public Postgres configuration and release/startup policy remain S10d-3,
as the parent RFC's S10d-2 implementation note records.

Renewal failure or the independent monotonic lease deadline latches ownership
loss. Queue renewal and public S10d-3 serving share `LeaseDeadline`: measure from
before renewal SQL, expire at the lease period minus min(50 ms, one quarter of
the period), and never revive expired authority on a late renewal completion.
The watchdog never locks the renewal connection; its sleep is at most 10 ms
and shortens to the remaining deadline for short leases. Command admission/start
also check the deadline. Startup retains a release guard until installation
succeeds, including renewal and thread-spawn failures. A renewal that commits
but then fails `renewed()` drops its lease without release, accepting up to one
extra lease period of unavailability rather than reviving expired authority.

`Writer::call` returns a typed `Result`; serving, adoption and worker supervision
handle refusals without panicking. All JSON serving doors use one transport
policy: `E_RATE_LIMIT` → **503**, `Retry-After: 1`, retryable;
`E_STORE_OWNERSHIP` → **409**, not retryable; pre-execution disconnect →
`E_COMMITTER_STOPPED`, **503**, retryable/not applied; worker death after start
or genuine ambiguous COMMIT → `E_COMMIT_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`, **500**, not retryable. As before,
a held change poll that already emitted HTTP 200 heartbeats reports later errors
in its final typed body. Bulk maintenance returns `Result<bool, RuntimeError>`
so refusal is logged and distinguished from idle work.

A view-once asset consumes its view when a full, non-range read starts (author
ruling, 2026-10-23; LLP 3000 §7). It commits consumption before sending any
response bytes, while retaining per-asset/principal transfer custody. Range
probes refuse with `E_ASSET_RANGE` without consumption; a request known to have
been aborted before admission consumes nothing. Refused or unknown bookkeeping
delivers no asset bytes. The first full GET returns 200 with the full body, and
later full GETs return 410 `E_ASSET_VIEWED`. A failed transport after the commit
still consumes the view, even if no response bytes arrive: one lost view is
accepted to keep bytes from escaping without durable consumption.
Regression witnesses fill the mailbox before delivery and verify a subsequent
fetch is refused, even after the mailbox drains. SQLite file and memory HTTP
witnesses also hold delivery after COMMIT, observe the consumed view before any
response bytes, then verify the complete body and subsequent refusal. A known
aborted pipelined request and range probes leave the view available. The
Postgres witness commits through the same engine method, then reopens the store
and verifies the duplicate refusal and independent principal. Its public HTTP
asset schemas and blob delivery remain excluded under the parent's §3.4 (P1b);
this ledger witness does not claim Postgres HTTP asset support.

After lease loss, new submissions refuse with
`SubmitError::OwnershipLost`, mapped to `E_STORE_OWNERSHIP` by serving.
An admission registry arbitrates command start against lease loss: every
waiting acknowledgement is refused immediately, without waiting for a busy
writer or an uncancellable COMMIT. Queued bodies subsequently drain without
execution. The admission ticket calls this transition `start_execution`, so the
unchanged lexical opener census cannot mistake it for a database `begin`.
Capacity-blocked control callers also observe the loss. A running
command retains its actual result; the unchanged Postgres first-lock token
fence still prevents writes after takeover. Physical COMMIT is never cancelled
or retried, and `CommitOutcomeUnknown` crosses the typed acknowledgement
unchanged. The worker-ledger conflict retains S10d-2's neutral path helper
inside an owned serving command.

Real-Postgres queue witnesses cover sixteen queued mutations and one
capacity-blocked caller refused while the active command remains paused,
independent renewal beyond the initial lease while COMMIT is blocked and the
queue is full, and a backend terminated during COMMIT returning the original
typed unknown outcome. The private-cluster script runs these witnesses. Their raw database fault
fixtures live under `store/`, inside the adapter test boundary; the queue
module retains only shared-port tests.

**Fix round 2 qualification completed (2026-10-23):** explicit cross-thread
request attribution repaired the earlier stopped checkpoint. Both unchanged RT0
tables matched all twelve warm rows, the adoption witnesses and full 29-case
lifecycle target passed, and every requested suite ran. The lane report retains
the reproducible unrelated full-CLI/device failures and evaluator Serializable
stress failure; this is scoped qualification, not a clean-full-suite claim.
K0-Q landed at `901a362705`; the third review's two P2s and four low-priority
findings are repaired by this fix-forward, with fresh checks recorded in the
JOURNAL.

The queue, concurrent HTTP, overload, full-suite and local W4 evidence are in
[the lane report](../snapback4/reports/20261022-k0q/REPORT.md). The SQLite
flush-per-member ceiling remains; batching is the next slice.

    22	fn write_opener(tokens: &[Token<'_>]) -> bool {

    69	fn production_sites(source: &str) -> BTreeSet<usize> {
    70	    let tokens = tokens(source);
    71	    let mut lines = BTreeSet::new();
    72	    let (mut depth, mut test_depth) = (0, None);
    73	    let (mut cfg_test, mut module) = (false, false);
    74	    for (i, token) in tokens.iter().enumerate() {
    75	        if starts(&tokens[i..], &["#", "[", "cfg", "(", "test", ")", "]"])
    76	            || starts(
    77	                &tokens[i..],
    78	                &["#", "[", "cfg", "(", "all", "(", "test", ","],
    79	            )
    80	        {
    81	            cfg_test = true;
    82	        }
    83	        if cfg_test && token.text == "mod" {
    84	            module = true;
    85	        }
    86	        if token.text == "{" {
    87	            depth += 1;
    88	            if module && test_depth.is_none() {
    89	                test_depth = Some(depth);
    90	            }
    91	            cfg_test = false;
    92	            module = false;
    93	        }
    94	        if test_depth.is_none() && write_opener(&tokens[i..]) {
    95	            lines.insert(token.line);
    96	        }
    97	        if token.text == "}" {
    98	            if test_depth == Some(depth) {
    99	                test_depth = None;
   100	            }
   101	            depth -= 1;
   102	        }
   103	        if matches!(token.text, ";" | "}") {
   104	            cfg_test = false;
   105	            module = false;
   106	        }
   107	    }
   108	    lines
   109	}

   111	fn walk(root: &Path, dir: &Path, counts: &mut Counts) {
   112	    for entry in fs::read_dir(dir).unwrap() {
   113	        let path = entry.unwrap().path();
   114	        let relative = path.strip_prefix(root).unwrap();
   115	        if excluded(relative) {

   117	        }
   118	        if path.is_dir() {
   119	            walk(root, &path, counts);
   120	        } else if path.extension().is_some_and(|ext| ext == "rs") && !test_file(relative) {
   121	            let lines = production_sites(&fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap());
   122	            if !lines.is_empty() {
   123	                counts.insert(relative.to_str().unwrap().replace('\\', "/"), lines.len());
   124	                if std::env::var("SNAPBACK4_COMMITTER_CENSUS").as_deref() == Ok("1") {
   125	                    eprintln!("{}: {:?}", relative.display(), lines);
   126	                }
   127	            }
   128	        }
   129	    }
   130	}

   132	fn read_allowlist(path: &Path) -> Counts {
   133	    let mut counts = Counts::new();
   134	    for line in fs::read_to_string(path)
   135	        .unwrap()
   136	        .lines()
   137	        .filter(|line| !line.trim().is_empty() && !line.starts_with('#'))
   138	    {
   139	        let fields: Vec<_> = line.split_whitespace().collect();
   140	        assert_eq!(fields.len(), 2, "invalid allowlist line: {line}");
   141	        let count: usize = fields[1].parse().expect("integer count");
   142	        assert!(count > 0, "remove zero entry: {line}");
   143	        assert!(
   144	            counts.insert(fields[0].into(), count).is_none(),
   145	            "duplicate entry: {line}"
   146	        );
   147	    }
   148	    counts
   149	}

   151	fn increases(actual: &Counts, allowed: &Counts) -> Vec<String> {

   153	        let limit = allowed.get(path).copied().unwrap_or(0);
   154	        if *count > limit {
   155	            Some(format!("{RULE}: {path}: {count} sites exceeds listed {limit}; route durable writes through the committer; this allowlist may only fall"))
   156	        } else {
   157	            None
   158	        }
   159	    }).collect()
   160	}

     1	//! Admission must survive closed and saturated operator output pipes.
     2	#![cfg(unix)]
     3	use std::{fs::{self, File}, io::{BufRead, BufReader, Read, Write}, net::TcpStream, os::fd::{AsRawFd, FromRawFd}, process::{Child, Command, Stdio}, sync::mpsc, thread, time::Duration};
     4	struct Host { child: Child, dir: std::path::PathBuf }
     5	impl Drop for Host { fn drop(&mut self) { let _ = self.child.kill(); let _ = self.child.wait(); let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(&self.dir); } }
     6	
     7	#[test]
     8	fn admission_survives_closed_stderr() { check(false); }
     9	#[test]
    10	fn admission_survives_full_stderr() { check(true); }
    11	fn check(full: bool) {
    12	        // Both cases hold 228 sockets; overlapping them exceeds macOS's
    13	        // ordinary 256-descriptor test-process limit before exercising the server.
    14	        static SOCKETS: std::sync::Mutex<()> = std::sync::Mutex::new(());
    15	        let _sockets = SOCKETS.lock().unwrap();
    16	        let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("sb4-overload-{}-{full}", std::process::id()));
    17	        fs::create_dir_all(dir.join("snapback")).unwrap();
    18	        fs::write(dir.join("snapback/schema.q"), "use personas alice\nquery hello():\n  return 1\n").unwrap();
    19	        let mut fds = [0; 2]; assert_eq!(unsafe { libc::pipe(fds.as_mut_ptr()) }, 0);
    20	        for fd in fds { assert_eq!(unsafe { libc::fcntl(fd, libc::F_SETFD, libc::FD_CLOEXEC) }, 0); }
    21	        let mut reader = unsafe { File::from_raw_fd(fds[0]) };
    22	        let mut writer = unsafe { File::from_raw_fd(fds[1]) };
    23	        let child = Command::new(std::env::var("SNAPBACK4_OVERLOAD_BIN").unwrap_or_else(|_| env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_snapback4").into()))
    24	            .args(["dev", "--fresh", "--replay", "--no-watch", "--port", "0", "--long-waits", "1", "--claim-waits", "1"]).current_dir(&dir)
    25	            .stdin(Stdio::null()).stdout(Stdio::piped()).stderr(writer.try_clone().unwrap()).spawn().unwrap();
    26	        let mut host = Host { child, dir };
    27	        let stdout = host.child.stdout.take().unwrap();
    28	        let (tx, rx) = mpsc::channel();
    29	        thread::spawn(move || { let mut line = String::new(); let _ = BufReader::new(stdout).read_line(&mut line); let _ = tx.send(line); });
    30	        let url = rx.recv_timeout(Duration::from_secs(10)).expect("startup");
    31	        let address = url.trim().strip_prefix("http://").expect("server URL");
    32	        // Drain only startup diagnostics, then explicitly fault the same pipe.
    33	        unsafe { libc::fcntl(reader.as_raw_fd(), libc::F_SETFL, libc::O_NONBLOCK); }
    34	        let mut buffer = [0; 4096]; while reader.read(&mut buffer).is_ok_and(|n| n > 0) {}
    35	        if full {
    36	            unsafe { libc::fcntl(writer.as_raw_fd(), libc::F_SETFL, libc::O_NONBLOCK); }
    37	            while writer.write(&buffer).is_ok() {}
    38	            // The child's descriptor shares these flags. Restore blocking mode:
    39	            // this is the original full-pipe failure, not a nonblocking fake.
    40	            unsafe { libc::fcntl(writer.as_raw_fd(), libc::F_SETFL, 0); }
    41	        } else { drop(reader); }
    42	        // The connection cap reserves 128 intake slots, 98 queued short requests,
    43	        // and the long-wait pools (here 1 + 1): 228 total.
    44	        let held: Vec<_> = (0..228).map(|_| TcpStream::connect(address).unwrap()).collect();
    45	        thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(100));
    46	        for i in 0..9 {
    47	            if i == 8 { thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(1250)); } // summary writer has attempted the faulty pipe
    48	            let mut extra = TcpStream::connect(address).unwrap();
    49	            extra.set_read_timeout(Some(Duration::from_secs(2))).unwrap();
    50	            let mut reply = String::new();
    51	            extra.read_to_string(&mut reply).expect("admission still answers with stderr unavailable");
    52	            assert!(reply.contains("503 Service Unavailable"), "full={full}, exit={:?}: {reply}", host.child.try_wait().unwrap());
    53	            assert!(reply.contains("\"retryable\":true"));
    54	        }
    55	        assert!(host.child.try_wait().unwrap().is_none(), "logging must not kill the server");
    56	        drop(held);
    57	        thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(150));
    58	        let mut recovered = TcpStream::connect(address).unwrap();
    59	        recovered.set_read_timeout(Some(Duration::from_secs(2))).unwrap();
    60	        recovered.write_all(b"GET /schema HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: localhost\r\nConnection: close\r\n\r\n").unwrap();
    61	        let mut reply = String::new(); recovered.read_to_string(&mut reply).unwrap();
    62	        assert!(reply.contains("401 Unauthorized"), "admitted request still completes: {reply}");
    63	}

    25	for (const tier of ["online", "web"]) test(`${tier} upload retries two HTTP admission refusals, then returns the admitted asset`, async t => {
    26	  const attempts = [], bodies = [];
    27	  const server = createServer(async (request, response) => {
    28	    const path = new URL(request.url, "http://localhost").pathname;
    29	    const reply = (body, status = 200) => { response.writeHead(status, { "content-type": "application/json" }); response.end(JSON.stringify(body)); };
    30	    if (path === "/schema") return reply({ generation: 1, schema: { tables: {} }, programs: [] });
    31	    if (path === "/sync") return reply({ snapshot: false, events: [], watermark: 0, more: false, generation: 1, acknowledged: [] });
    32	    if (path === "/changes") return; // Close with the client/server; never send a fake change.
    33	    if (path === "/probe") return reply({ ok: true });
    34	    if (path !== "/assets") return reply({}, 404);
    35	    attempts.push(Date.now());
    36	    const chunks = [];
    37	    for await (const chunk of request) chunks.push(chunk);
    38	    bodies.push(Buffer.concat(chunks));
    39	    assert.equal(request.headers["x-snapback-persona"], "alice");
    40	    if (attempts.length <= 2) {
    41	      response.setHeader("Retry-After", "0");
    42	      return reply({ denied: rateLimit }, 429);
    43	    }
    44	    reply({ asset });
    45	  });
    46	  server.listen(0, "127.0.0.1"); await once(server, "listening");
    47	  t.after(() => { server.closeAllConnections(); return new Promise(resolve => server.close(resolve)); });
    48	  const options = { url: `http://127.0.0.1:${server.address().port}`, persona: "alice" };
    49	  const client = tier === "online" ? createClient(options) : await createLocalClient({ ...options, device: webDevice({ wasm, persist: memoryPersistence() }) });
    50	  t.after(() => client.close());
    51	  const uploaded = await client.upload(bytes);
    52	  assert.deepEqual(uploaded, { ok: true, asset });
    53	  assert.equal(attempts.length, 3);
    54	  assert.ok(attempts[1] - attempts[0] >= 245, "first retry backs off");
    55	  assert.ok(attempts[2] - attempts[1] >= 495, "second retry increases backoff");
    56	  for (const body of bodies) assert.deepEqual(body, Buffer.from(bytes));
    57	});

   155	for (const capacity of ["connection", "worker"]) test(`fresh replay host reports ${capacity} overload to the real upload transport and recovers`, { timeout: hangBudget(11) }, async t => {
   156	  const dir = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), "sb4-upload-overload-"));
   157	  await mkdir(join(dir, "snapback"));
   158	  await writeFile(join(dir, "snapback/schema.q"), `use personas alice
   159	table photos:
   160	  photo: image <=10000
   161	  read <- allow
   162	`);
   163	  const binary = process.env.SNAPBACK4_BIN ?? fileURLToPath(new URL("../../../target/debug/snapback4", import.meta.url));
   164	  // Smallest long-wait pools: the connection cap is 128 + 98 queued short slots + 1 + 1 (LLP 3000.009.001.000.001 §13).
   165	  const child = spawn(binary, ["dev", "--fresh", "--replay", "--no-watch", "--port", "0", "--long-waits", "1", "--claim-waits", "1"], { cwd: dir, stdio: ["ignore", "pipe", "pipe"] });
   166	  const exited = once(child, "exit");
   167	  let log = "";
   168	  child.stderr.on("data", bytes => { log += bytes; });
   169	  const sockets = [];
   170	  t.after(async () => {
   171	    for (const socket of sockets) socket.destroy();
   172	    child.kill("SIGTERM"); await exited;
   173	    await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true });
   174	  });
   175	  const url = await new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
   176	    let output = "";
   177	    const timeout = setTimeout(() => reject(Error(`server startup timeout: ${log}`)), hangBudget());
   178	    child.stdout.on("data", bytes => {
   179	      output += bytes;
   180	      const match = output.match(/http:\/\/127\.0\.0\.1:\d+(?=\r?\n)/);
   181	      if (match) { clearTimeout(timeout); resolve(match[0]); }
   182	    });
   183	    child.once("exit", () => { clearTimeout(timeout); reject(Error(`server exited: ${log}`)); });
   184	  });
   185	  const port = Number(new URL(url).port);
   186	  // Slow headers fill all 228 connection leases; 100-continue witnesses prove that
   187	  // unfinished mutations occupy all 32 workers before the upload is attempted.
   188	  await Promise.all(Array.from({ length: capacity === "connection" ? 228 : 32 }, async () => {
   189	    const socket = connect(port, "127.0.0.1");
   190	    socket.on("error", () => {});
   191	    sockets.push(socket);
   192	    await once(socket, "connect");
   193	    if (capacity === "worker") {
   194	      let header = "";
   195	      const onData = bytes => { header += bytes; };
   196	      socket.on("data", onData);
   197	      const ready = waitFor(() => header.includes("\r\n") && [Buffer.from(header)], "complete 100-continue status line");
   198	      socket.write("POST /m/held HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: localhost\r\nx-snapback-persona: alice\r\nContent-Length: 100000\r\nExpect: 100-continue\r\n\r\n");
   199	      try { assert.match(String((await ready)[0]), /HTTP\/1.1 100 Continue/); }
   200	      finally { socket.off("data", onData); }
   201	    }
   202	  }));
   203	  const png = Buffer.alloc(2081);
   204	  Buffer.from("89504e470d0a1a0a0000000d494844520000000100000001", "hex").copy(png);
   205	  let attempts = 0;
   206	  const result = await uploadAsset(url, { "x-snapback-persona": "alice" }, (...args) => { attempts++; return defaultFetch(...args); }, png);
   207	  assert.equal(attempts, 4, "the actual server refusal must be retryable");
   208	  assert.equal(result.ok, false, log);
   209	  assert.equal(result.why.code, "E_RATE_LIMIT", JSON.stringify(result));
   210	  assert.match(result.why.message, /HTTP connections/);
   211	  const diagnostic = capacity === "connection" ? /E_RATE_LIMIT at http: all 228 HTTP connections/ : /E_RATE_LIMIT at workers: cumulative refused=\[0, [1-9]/;
   212	  await waitFor(() => log.split("\n").slice(0, -1).some(line => diagnostic.test(line)), "complete overload diagnostic");
   213	  assert.match(log, diagnostic);

   215	  for (const socket of sockets) socket.destroy();
   216	  const recovered = await uploadAsset(url, { "x-snapback-persona": "alice" }, defaultFetch, png);
   217	  assert.equal(recovered.ok, true, JSON.stringify(recovered));
   218	});

codex
The new helper passes both writer choices into the existing member driver. Authority validation and fenced replay still run inside the member; entropy and publication still wait for physical COMMIT. I’m checking the PostgreSQL framing and saturation fixtures for any behavior change at those boundaries.

   559	    /// Fold a driver row stream without retaining an unbounded result vector.
   560	    pub(super) fn query_fold<T>(&self, sql: &'static str, params: &[&(dyn ToSql + Sync)], mut total: T, mut fold: impl FnMut(T, Row) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
   561	        use postgres::fallible_iterator::FallibleIterator;
   562	        let statement = self.prepared(sql)?;
   563	        self.guard_params(&statement,params)?;
   564	        self.calls.set(self.calls.get() + 1);
   565	        let mut inner = self.inner.borrow_mut();
   566	        inner.before_bind(self.calls)?;
   567	        let mut rows = inner.client.query_raw(&statement, params.iter().copied())?;
   568	        while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {
   569	            total = fold(total, Row::Bind(row))?;
   570	        }
   571	        Ok(total)
   572	    }

   574	    pub(crate) fn execute(&self, sql: &'static str, params: &[&(dyn ToSql + Sync)]) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
   575	        let statement = self.prepared(sql)?;
   576	        if statement.params().iter().all(|t| super::literal::name(t).is_ok())
   577	            && statement.columns().iter().all(|c| super::flight::text_type(c.type_())) {
   578	            return Ok(self.flight(&[(sql, params)], "")?.remove(0).count);
   579	        }
   580	        self.guard_params(&statement, params)?;
   581	        let mut inner = self.inner.borrow_mut();
   582	        inner.before_bind(self.calls)?;
   583	        self.calls.set(self.calls.get() + 1);
   584	        Ok(inner.client.execute(&statement, params)?)
   585	    }
   586	
   587	    // Unconditional inserts/upserts must not acknowledge a suppressed write.
   588	    pub(super) fn execute_one(&self, sql: &'static str, params: &[&(dyn ToSql + Sync)]) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   589	        let rows = self.execute(sql, params)?;
   590	        if rows != 1 {
   591	            return Err(RuntimeError::Store(format!("expected one affected row, got {rows}")));
   592	        }
   593	        Ok(())
   594	    }
   595	
   596	    pub(crate) fn batch(&self, sql: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   597	        self.ensure_active()?;
   598	        if sql.split(|c: char| !c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() && c != '_').any(|token| token.eq_ignore_ascii_case("ROLLBACK")) {
   599	            return Err(RuntimeError::Store("use the explicit transaction/savepoint rollback method".into()));
   600	        }
   601	        self.calls.set(self.calls.get() + 1);
   602	        self.inner.borrow_mut().simple_query(sql)?;
   603	        Ok(())
   604	    }
   605	
   606	    pub(super) fn rollback_named(&self, name: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   607	        self.ensure_active()?;
   608	        self.rows_cache.borrow_mut().clear();
   609	        self.calls.set(self.calls.get() + 1);
   610	        self.inner.borrow_mut().simple_query(&format!("ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT {name}; RELEASE SAVEPOINT {name}"))?;
   611	        if self.read_sessions_installed.get() {
   612	            self.read_sessions_installed.set(super::identity::read_sessions_present(self)?);
   613	        }
   614	        Ok(())
   615	    }
   616	
   617	    pub fn rollback_program(&self) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   618	        if self.savepoints.borrow().iter().any(|(name, _)| name == "program") { self.rollback_to("program") }
   619	        else { self.rollback_named("program") }
   620	    }
   621	
   622	    pub(super) fn rollback_prefetch(&self) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   623	        self.ensure_active()?;
   624	        self.calls.set(self.calls.get() + 1);
   625	        self.inner.borrow_mut().simple_query("ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT prefetch; RELEASE SAVEPOINT prefetch")?;
   626	        Ok(())
   627	    }
   628	
   629	    pub fn commit(self) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   630	        self.ensure_active()?;
   631	        if self.inner.borrow().done { return Ok(()); }
   632	        self.flush_tail()?;
   633	        let mut inner = self.inner.into_inner();
   634	        if !self.commit_settings.get() {
   635	            inner.pending.push_str("SET LOCAL statement_timeout = 0; SET LOCAL lock_timeout = 0;");
   636	        }
   637	        inner.flush(self.calls)?;
   638	        self.calls.set(self.calls.get()+1);
   639	        // The last data flight verified the codec settings. Only client-side
   640	        // validation intervenes; COMMIT stays alone and decodes no row payload.
   641	        // Once COMMIT is sent, even transport loss must retain unknown-outcome classification.
   642	        inner.done = true;
   643	        super::ownership::commit_prepared(inner.client,self.commit_warning)?;
   644	        if self.read_sessions_installed.get() { self.read_sessions.set(true); }
   645	        Ok(())
   646	    }
   647	
   648	    pub fn rollback(self) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   649	        if self.inner.borrow().done { return Ok(()); }
   650	        self.calls.set(self.calls.get() + 1);
   651	        let mut inner = self.inner.into_inner();
   652	        inner.flush(self.calls)?;
   653	        inner.client.batch_execute("ROLLBACK")?;
   654	        inner.done = true;
   655	        Ok(())
   656	    }
   657	
   658	    pub fn record_commit(
   659	        &self,
   660	        seq: u64,
   661	        at: i64,
   662	        program: &str,
   663	        principal: &str,
   664	        kind: &str,
   665	        touched: &[String],
   666	        writes: u64,
   667	    ) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   668	        self.ensure_active()?;
   669	        signed(seq)?; signed(writes)?;
   670	        let mut tail = self.tail.borrow_mut();
   671	        if tail.commit.is_some() { return Err(RuntimeError::Store("commit tail slot already occupied".into())); }
   672	        // @ref LLP 3000.009.001.000#33-extraction-first-with-no-behavior-change — P1-CE uses SQLite's recorder and coverage fallback, in the existing tail flight.
   673	        if !self.changes.borrow().covers(touched) { self.changes.borrow_mut().widen(); }
   674	        tail.commit = Some(super::tail::Commit {
   675	            seq, at, program: program.into(), principal: principal.into(), kind: kind.into(),
   676	            touched: canonical_json(&touched), writes, footprint: canonical_json(&self.changes.borrow().footprint()),
   677	        });
   678	        Ok(())
   679	    }

   102	
   103	pub(super) fn connect(mut config: Config) -> Result<(Client, Arc<AtomicBool>), RuntimeError> {
   104	    if config.get_connect_timeout().is_none() {
   105	        config.connect_timeout(Duration::from_secs(5));
   106	    }
   107	    let warning = Arc::new(AtomicBool::new(false));
   108	    let seen = warning.clone();
   109	    config.notice_callback(move |notice| {
   110	        // SQLSTATE is stable across lc_messages; localized warning text
   111	        // must never turn a cancelled synchronous wait into acknowledgement.
   112	        if notice.code() == &SqlState::QUERY_CANCELED {
   113	            seen.store(true, Ordering::SeqCst);
   114	        }
   115	    });
   116	    #[cfg(any(test, feature = "test-support"))]
   117	    crate::round_trips::postgres(1); // startup through ReadyForQuery
   118	    let mut client = config.connect(NoTls)?;
   119	    super::literal::configure(&mut client)?;
   120	    #[cfg(any(test, feature = "test-support"))]
   121	    crate::round_trips::postgres(3); // settings batch + verify prepare/execute
   122	    // @ref LLP 3000.009.001.000#8 — every serving/lease/reader connection needs session affinity.
   123	    #[cfg(any(test, feature = "test-support"))]
   124	    crate::round_trips::postgres(2); // implicit prepare + execute
   125	    let first = client.query_one("SELECT pg_backend_pid(),current_setting('application_name'),current_setting('server_version_num')::int", &[])?;
   126	    #[cfg(any(test, feature = "test-support"))]
   127	    crate::round_trips::postgres(2);
   128	    let second = client.query_one("SELECT pg_backend_pid(),current_setting('application_name')", &[])?;
   129	    if first.get::<_, i32>(0) != second.get::<_, i32>(0) || first.get::<_, String>(1) != second.get::<_, String>(1) {
   130	        return Err(RuntimeError::StoreUnsupported { family: "transaction-pooling" });
   131	    }
   132	    if !(160000..180000).contains(&first.get::<_, i32>(2)) { return Err(RuntimeError::StoreUnsupported { family: "postgres-version" }); }
   133	    Ok((client, warning))
   134	}
   135	
   136	// @ref LLP 3000.009.001.000#5.4.1 — COMMIT has no adapter timeout or cancellation; loss of acknowledgement is unknown.
   137	pub(super) fn commit(mut tx: Transaction<'_>, warning: &AtomicBool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   138	    tx.batch_execute("SET LOCAL statement_timeout = 0; SET LOCAL lock_timeout = 0")?;
   139	    warning.store(false, Ordering::SeqCst);
   140	    commit_result(tx.commit(), warning)
   141	}
   142	pub(super) fn commit_prepared(client: &mut Client, warning: &AtomicBool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   143	    warning.store(false, Ordering::SeqCst);
   144	    commit_result(client.batch_execute("COMMIT"), warning)
   145	}
   146	
   147	fn commit_result(result: Result<(), postgres::Error>, warning: &AtomicBool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   148	    if warning.swap(false, Ordering::SeqCst) {
   149	        return Err(RuntimeError::CommitOutcomeUnknown(
   150	            "COMMIT returned a cancellation warning".into(),
   151	        ));
   152	    }
   153	    result.map_err(|error| {
   154	        // Explicit abort replies establish rollback. Transport loss, backend
   155	        // termination and other unclassified COMMIT failures do not.
   156	        if error.code().is_some_and(|code| {
   157	            matches!(code.code(), "40001" | "40P01") || code.code().starts_with("23")
   158	        }) {
   159	            RuntimeError::Postgres(error)
   160	        } else {
   161	            RuntimeError::CommitOutcomeUnknown(error.to_string())
   162	        }
   163	    })
   164	}
   165	
   166	fn verify(tx: &Tx<'_>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   167	    shape::verify(
   168	        tx,
   169	        "_owner",
   170	        &[
   171	            ("singleton", "boolean", true, false),
   172	            ("store_id", "text", true, true),
   173	            ("holder", "text", true, true),
   174	            ("token", "bigint", true, false),
   175	            ("lease_until", "timestamp with time zone", true, false),
   176	        ],
   177	        &["singleton"],
   178	        "E_STORE_OWNERSHIP",
   179	    )
   180	}

    63	/// Evaluation modes, not a claim of horizontally scalable commit ordering.
    64	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
    65	pub enum WriteMode {
    66	    /// Reserve the head before executing, matching SQLite's single writer.
    67	    #[default]
    68	    Serialized,
    69	    /// Run concurrently, retrying the entire transaction on serialization
    70	    /// failure. A single global sync head is still a contention point.
    71	    Serializable,
    72	}

   104	    fn begin(&mut self, mode: Option<WriteMode>, opening: bool) -> Result<Tx<'_>, RuntimeError> {
   105	        if self.read_only && mode.is_some() { return Err(RuntimeError::Store("Postgres reader is read-only".into())); }
   106	        if !self.qualified {
   107	            super::literal::configure(&mut self.client)?;
   108	            self.calls.set(self.calls.get() + 3);
   109	            self.qualified = true;
   110	        }
   111	        self.page_reads.set(0);
   112	        let begin = match mode {
   113	            None => "BEGIN ISOLATION LEVEL REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY;",
   114	            Some(WriteMode::Serialized) => "BEGIN ISOLATION LEVEL READ COMMITTED READ WRITE;",
   115	            Some(WriteMode::Serializable) => "BEGIN ISOLATION LEVEL SERIALIZABLE READ WRITE;",
   116	        };
   117	        let inner = super::flight::Session { client: &mut self.client, pending: begin.into(), started: false, done: false, settings_refused: false, checks: Vec::new() };

   120	            rows_cache: RefCell::new(super::prefetch::Cache::default()), prefetch_enabled: self.prefetch, head: Cell::new(None), serialized_head: Cell::new(false), tail: RefCell::new(super::tail::Tail::default()), changes: Default::default(), savepoints: RefCell::new(Vec::new()) };

   122	            // Bootstrap is the sole exception: an absent owner relation is
   123	            // installed in the successful opening transaction, never by readers.
   124	            let mut exists = !opening || tx.query("SELECT to_regclass('_owner') IS NOT NULL", &[])?[0].get::<_, bool>(0);
   125	            if !exists {
   126	                if self.fence.as_ref().is_some_and(|fence| fence.token != 0) {
   127	                    return Err(RuntimeError::StoreOwnership("acquired owner record disappeared; refuse to bootstrap over lost ownership".into()));
   128	                }
   129	                // @ref LLP 3000.009.001.000#3.4 — legacy owner installation is atomic with attach and excludes a live evaluator.
   130	                // Guard legacy namespaces even when no logical id exists yet.
   131	                // Fresh creation has no backend relation and takes no lock.
   132	                tx.query("SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('sb4-owner-bootstrap:' || current_schema(),0)) WHERE to_regclass('_backend') IS NOT NULL", &[])?;
   133	                #[cfg(test)]
   134	                super::ownership::test_hooks::run(super::ownership::test_hooks::Point::AfterBootstrapLock);
   135	                if let Some(fence) = &self.fence {
   136	                    super::ownership::install(&tx, &fence.store_id)?;
   137	                    exists = true;
   138	                }
   139	            }
   140	            if exists {
   141	                // @ref LLP 3000.009.001.000#5.1 — first row lock, before head, generation or any family write.
   142	                let rows = tx.query("SELECT store_id,token FROM _owner WHERE singleton FOR KEY SHARE", &[]).map_err(|error| {
   143	                    if matches!(&error, RuntimeError::Postgres(e) if e.code() == Some(&postgres::error::SqlState::T_R_SERIALIZATION_FAILURE)) {
   144	                        RuntimeError::StoreOwnership("owner changed in this transaction snapshot".into())
   145	                    } else { error }
   146	                })?;
   147	                let expected = self.fence.as_ref().map_or(0, |f| f.token);
   148	                if rows.len() != 1 || rows[0].get::<_, i64>(1) != expected
   149	                    || self.fence.as_ref().is_some_and(|f| rows[0].get::<_, &str>(0) != f.store_id) {
   150	                    return Err(RuntimeError::StoreOwnership("stale owner token or logical store identity".into()));
   151	                }
   152	            }
   153	        }
   154	        Ok(tx)
   155	    }

   158	    pub(crate) fn ingress_session_authority_facts(&mut self, digest: &str) -> Result<crate::identity::facts::SessionFacts, RuntimeError> {
   159	        if absent_text_key(&[digest]) { return Ok(Default::default()); }
   160	        let sql = super::authority::session_facts_sql(self.read_sessions.get());
   161	        let statement = if let Some(statement) = self.cache.get(sql) { statement.clone() } else {
   162	            self.calls.set(self.calls.get() + 1);

   166	        guard_text_params(&statement, &[&digest])?;
   167	        self.calls.set(self.calls.get() + 1);

   170	        // All discovery and any read-arm retry then share a dedicated snapshot.
   171	        if !self.read_sessions.get() && facts.sessions.is_empty() {
   172	            let (tx, facts) = self.session_authority_transaction(digest)?;
   173	            tx.rollback()?;
   174	            return Ok(facts);
   175	        }

   280	pub(crate) fn listener_error(error: std::io::Error) -> String {
   281	    let mut message = format!("E_HTTP_LISTENER: HTTP listener stopped: {error}");
   282	    #[cfg(unix)]
   283	    if matches!(error.raw_os_error(), Some(libc::EMFILE | libc::ENFILE)) {
   284	        message.push_str("; descriptor exhaustion: run `snapback4 doctor`, reduce open connections/files or raise the shell/container/service NOFILE limit, then restart dev");
   285	    }
   286	    message
   287	}

                cursor += 1
            if line[0] in ' +':
                out.append(line[1:])
            i += 1
    out.extend(original[cursor:])
    expected = ''.join(out).encode()
    assert expected == (preview/path).read_bytes(), path
    assert ''.join(original).encode() == (repo/path).read_bytes(), path
    verified += 1
    print('Verified baseline + patch = copied file:', path)

   416	    impl CommitTransaction for Transaction<'_> {
   417	        fn commit_transaction(self) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {

   419	            if self.1 {

   436	                        Ok(Transaction(events, true))
   437	                    })?;
   438	                    events.borrow_mut().push("body");

     1	//! SQLite witnesses for the member boundary, independent of engine producers.
     2	use super::*;
     3	use crate::rows::Rows;
     4	
     5	impl MemberTx<'_, '_> {
     6	    pub(crate) fn meta(&self, key: &str) -> Result<Option<String>, RuntimeError> {
     7	        self.tx.test_support_meta(key)
     8	    }
     9	    pub(crate) fn set_meta(&self, key: &str, value: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {

    14	#[test]
    15	fn rt3b_bound_receipt_hit_aborts_only_its_member_sqlite() {
    16	    bound_replay_keeps_other_members(AnyStore::memory().unwrap());
    17	}
    18	
    19	// @ref LLP 3000.009.001.000.001#7-correctness-constraints — serial replay aborts only its member, preserving earlier and later members until COMMIT.
    20	pub(super) fn bound_replay_keeps_other_members(mut store: AnyStore) {
    21	    let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
    22	    let args = serde_json::json!({"n": 1});
    23	    Committer::execute(&tx, Member::new(Kind::Program, |member| {
    24	        member.set_schedule_boundary("rt3b", 10)?;
    25	        member.record_commit(1, 100, ("change", "alice", "mutation"), &[], 0)?;
    26	        member.record_applied("alice", "same-id", 1, "stored outcome")?;
    27	        member.bind_applied("alice", "same-id", "change", &args, 100)?;
    28	        member.complete(())
    29	    })).unwrap();
    30	    let replay = Committer::execute(&tx, Member::new(Kind::Program, |member| {
    31	        member.set_schedule_boundary("rt3b", 20)?;
    32	        let stored = member.replay_bound("alice", "same-id", "change", &args)?.expect("earlier member owns the id");
    33	        member.rollback(stored)
    34	    })).unwrap();
    35	    assert_eq!(replay.end, Frame::Abort);
    36	    assert_eq!(replay.value, "stored outcome");
    37	    assert_eq!(tx.next_seq().unwrap(), 2);
    38	    Committer::execute(&tx, Member::new(Kind::Program, |member| {
    39	        member.record_commit(2, 200, ("later", "alice", "mutation"), &[], 0)?;
    40	        member.complete(())
    41	    })).unwrap();
    42	    tx.commit().unwrap();
    43	    assert_eq!(store.seq().unwrap(), 2);
    44	    assert_eq!(store.schedule_boundary("rt3b").unwrap(), Some(10));
    45	    assert_eq!(store.applied_outcome("alice", "same-id").unwrap().as_deref(), Some("stored outcome"));
    46	}

    48	#[test]
    49	fn two_members_keep_the_first_on_second_rollback_drop_or_error() {
    50	    for mode in ["rollback", "drop", "error"] {
    51	        let mut store = AnyStore::memory().unwrap();
    52	        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
    53	        let first = Committer::execute(
    54	            &tx,
    55	            Member::new(Kind::Program, |member| {
    56	                member.set_meta("kept", "first")?;
    57	                member.pin_time_audience_clock(10)?;
    58	                // Exercise all existing nested frame names, including lazy
    59	                // sequence-table creation that must be invalidated on rollback.
    60	                for name in ["program", "job_chunk", "timeline_chunk", "seed_sequences"] {
    61	                    member.savepoint(name)?;
    62	                    member.set_meta("nested_discard", name)?;
    63	                    member.set_sequence("parents", "p", 99)?;
    64	                    member.rollback_to(name)?;
    65	                    assert_eq!(member.meta("nested_discard")?, None);
    66	                    assert_eq!(member.sequence_value("parents", "p")?, 0);
    67	                    member.savepoint(name)?;
    68	                    member.set_meta(name, "kept")?;
    69	                    member.release(name)?;
    70	                }
    71	                // Keep a released nonzero counter across the next member.
    72	                member.set_sequence("parents", "kept", 7)?;
    73	                member.complete(1)
    74	            }),
    75	        )
    76	        .unwrap();
    77	        assert!(matches!(
    78	            first,
    79	            Disposition {
    80	                end: Frame::Complete,
    81	                value: 1
    82	            }
    83	        ));
    84	        assert!(
    85	            !(&tx).test_support_conn().is_autocommit(),
    86	            "RELEASE is not a physical commit"
    87	        );
    88	        tx.widen_change_evidence();
    89	        assert!(tx.change_footprint().unknown);
    90	        let second = Committer::execute(
    91	            &tx,
    92	            Member::new(Kind::Engine, |member| {
    93	                assert!(!member.change_footprint().unknown);
    94	                assert_eq!(member.meta("kept")?.as_deref(), Some("first"));
    95	                member.set_meta("kept", "overwritten")?;
    96	                member.set_meta("discarded", "second")?;
    97	                member.pin_time_audience_clock(20)?;
    98	                member.set_sequence("parents", "p", 100)?;
    99	                member.set_sequence("parents", "kept", 100)?;
   100	                // Leave an inner frame open: member rollback must discard it too.
   101	                member.savepoint("job_chunk")?;
   102	                member.set_meta("inner_discarded", "second")?;
   103	                match mode {
   104	                    "rollback" => member.rollback(2),
   105	                    "drop" => {
   106	                        drop(member);
   107	                        Ok(Disposition {
   108	                            end: Frame::Abort,
   109	                            value: 2,
   110	                        })
   111	                    }
   112	                    _ => Err(RuntimeError::Store("body failed".into())),
   113	                }
   114	            }),
   115	        );
   116	        if mode == "error" {
   117	            assert!(second.is_err());
   118	        } else {
   119	            assert!(matches!(
   120	                second.unwrap(),
   121	                Disposition {
   122	                    end: Frame::Abort,
   123	                    value: 2
   124	                }
   125	            ));
   126	        }
   127	        assert!(!(&tx).test_support_conn().is_autocommit());
   128	        assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("parents", "p").unwrap(), 0);
   129	        assert_eq!(tx.sequence_value("parents", "kept").unwrap(), 7);
   130	        // The counter released by member 1 survives member 2's rollback.
   131	        // Inner frame rollbacks above separately exercise lazy-table repair.
   132	        tx.set_sequence("parents", "p", 7).unwrap();
   133	        tx.commit().unwrap();
   134	        assert_eq!((&store).test_support_meta("kept").unwrap().as_deref(), Some("first"));
   135	        assert_eq!(
   136	            (&store).test_support_meta("time_audience_clock").unwrap().as_deref(),
   137	            Some("10")
   138	        );
   139	        for key in ["discarded", "inner_discarded", "nested_discard"] {
   140	            assert_eq!((&store).test_support_meta(key).unwrap(), None, "{mode}: {key}");
   141	        }
   142	        for key in ["program", "job_chunk", "timeline_chunk", "seed_sequences"] {
   143	            assert_eq!((&store).test_support_meta(key).unwrap().as_deref(), Some("kept"));
   144	        }
   145	    }
   146	}

   149	fn normal_abort_and_error_discard_pins_and_metadata_in_the_solo_driver() {
   150	    for mode in ["rollback", "drop", "error"] {
   151	        let mut store = AnyStore::memory().unwrap();
   152	        let result = Committer::run(
   153	            &mut store,
   154	            Member::new(Kind::Direct, |tx| {
   155	                tx.pin_time_audience_clock(30)?;
   156	                tx.set_meta("discarded", "body")?;
   157	                match mode {
   158	                    "rollback" => tx.rollback(7),
   159	                    "drop" => tx.abort(7),
   160	                    _ => Err(RuntimeError::Store("body failed".into())),
   161	                }
   162	            }),
   163	        );
   164	        assert_eq!(result.is_ok(), mode != "error");
   165	        assert!((&store).test_support_conn().is_autocommit());
   166	        assert_eq!((&store).test_support_meta("time_audience_clock").unwrap(), None);
   167	        assert_eq!((&store).test_support_meta("discarded").unwrap(), None);
   168	    }

   171	#[test]
   172	fn failed_abort_keeps_the_rollback_cause_and_runs_no_continuation() {
   173	    for code in [rusqlite::ffi::SQLITE_BUSY, rusqlite::ffi::SQLITE_IOERR] {
   174	        for explicit in [false, true] {
   175	            let failure = || {
   176	                rusqlite::Error::SqliteFailure(
   177	                    rusqlite::ffi::Error::new(code),
   178	                    Some("injected member rollback failure".into()),
   179	                )
   180	            };
   181	            let expected = RuntimeError::Sql(failure());
   182	            let mut store = AnyStore::memory().unwrap();
   183	            let mut continued = false;
   184	            let error = Committer::run_with(
   185	                &mut store,
   186	                |store| {
   187	                    Committer::run_pending(
   188	                        store,
   189	                        Member::new(Kind::Engine, |tx| {
   190	                            tx.set_meta("failed_abort", "discard")?;
   191	                            tx.pin_time_audience_clock(30)?;
   192	                            tx.rollback_failure.set(Some(RuntimeError::Sql(failure())));
   193	                            if explicit {
   194	                                tx.rollback(Pending::value(7))
   195	                            } else {
   196	                                tx.abort(Pending::value(7))
   197	                            }
   198	                        }),
   199	                    )
   200	                },
   201	                |_, (), value| {
   202	                    continued = true;
   203	                    Ok(value)
   204	                },
   205	            )
   206	            .unwrap_err();
   207	            assert!(matches!(&error, RuntimeError::Sql(source)
   208	                if source.sqlite_error().unwrap().extended_code == code));
   209	            assert!(
   210	                std::error::Error::source(&error)
   211	                    .unwrap()
   212	                    .is::<rusqlite::Error>()
   213	            );
   214	            assert_eq!(error.to_string(), expected.to_string());
   215	            assert_eq!(error.refusal(), expected.refusal());
   216	            assert_eq!(error.is_busy(), code == rusqlite::ffi::SQLITE_BUSY);
   217	            assert!(!continued);
   218	            assert!((&store).test_support_conn().is_autocommit());
   219	            assert_eq!((&store).test_support_meta("failed_abort").unwrap(), None);
   220	            assert_eq!((&store).test_support_meta("time_audience_clock").unwrap(), None);
   221	        }
   222	    }
   223	}
